<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We disprove the unrestricted nearby Lagrangian conjecture. For some sufficiently large even integer <i>N</i>, we construct a closed exact smoothly embedded Lagrangian in $`T^*(S^9\times S^{N-1})`$ that is diffeomorphic to the base but is not Hamiltonian isotopic to the zero section.

- **claim-main-counterexample** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For some sufficiently large even integer $N$, the manifold $Q=S^9\times S^{N-1}$ admits a closed exact smoothly embedded Lagrangian $L\subset T^*Q$ such that $L$ is diffeomorphic to $Q$ and no compactly supported Hamiltonian isotopy carries the zero section to $L$.
  - Explanation: For some sufficiently large even N, the product of spheres Q = S^9 x S^(N-1) carries a closed exact embedded Lagrangian in its cotangent bundle that is diffeomorphic to Q but is not reached from the zero section by any compactly supported Hamiltonian isotopy. This disproves the unrestricted nearby Lagrangian conjecture.
- **claim-tube-fibration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-tube-fibration)): For the stable smooth tube space <a href="#eq:stable-tubes" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stable-tubes">[eq:stable-tubes]</a> there is a homotopy fiber sequence $$\label{eq:tube-fibration} H^s(*)\longrightarrow\mathbf T\xrightarrow{\gamma}BG.$$ The rigid tube map $\iota:BO\to\mathbf T$, defined by nondegenerate quadratic forms, satisfies $\gamma\circ\iota\simeq BJ$, where $BJ:BO\to BG$ is the stable spherical fibration map.
  - Explanation: Waldhausen's tube fibration: the stable smooth tube space maps to BG by the negative-domain spherical fibration, and the homotopy fiber of this map is the stable h-cobordism space of a point. The rigid map from BO composed with gamma agrees with the stable spherical fibration map BJ.
- **claim-kernel-class** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-kernel-class)): There exists $$\label{eq:kernel-class} 0\ne a\in\pi_9\mathbf T,\qquad \gamma_*a=0.$$ Moreover, $(BJ)_*:\pi_9BO\to\pi_9BG$ is injective.
  - Explanation: The ninth homotopy group of the stable tube space contains a nonzero class whose image in BG vanishes. It comes from the boundary map in the long exact sequence of the tube fibration failing to be onto. The stable J-homomorphism in this degree is injective.
- **claim-localized-family** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-localized-family)): Fix $B=S^9$ and a standard height function $\theta:B\to\mathbb R$. The class $a$ in <a href="#eq:kernel-class" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:kernel-class">[eq:kernel-class]</a> has, for arbitrarily large $m\geq20$, a representative $b\mapsto h_b$ in $T_{m,m}$ such that $(b,u)\mapsto h_b(u)$ is smooth and $h_b=q_{m,m}\|_{S^{2m-1}}$ on neighborhoods of both critical points of $\theta$. Its homogeneous extension $g_b$ satisfies, for constants $0<c\leq C<\infty$, $$\label{eq:uniform-homogeneous-gradient} c\lVert w\rVert\leq\lVert\nabla_wg_b(w)\rVert\leq C\lVert w\rVert \qquad(b\in B,\ w\in\mathbb R^{2m}).$$ For any fixed metric on $B$, there is also a constant $C_1$ such that $$\label{eq:base-mixed-bound} \lVert d_wd_bg_b(w)\rVert\leq C_1\lVert w\rVert.$$ The derivatives in <a href="#eq:base-mixed-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:base-mixed-bound">[eq:base-mixed-bound]</a> extend continuously to $w=0$ with value zero.
  - Explanation: The kernel class is realized, at an arbitrarily large finite stage of the telescope, by a smooth family of angular functions over S^9. The family is the standard quadratic one near both critical points of the height function and has two-sided linear gradient bounds and a linear bound on the mixed derivative.
- **claim-one-critical-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-one-critical)): Let $B=S^9$ and let $p\geq20$. Suppose that $f_b:\mathbb R^{2p}\to\mathbb R$, $b\in B$, is a smooth family satisfying the following conditions:* 1. *it is homogeneous at infinity, with smooth homogeneous data in $T_{p,p}$;* 2. *each $f_b$ has exactly one critical point, which is nondegenerate and varies smoothly with $b$;* 3. *the stable spherical class of the family at infinity is zero in $[B,BG]$.* *Then its stable tube class is zero in $[B,\mathbf T]$.
  - Explanation: A family over S^9 that is homogeneous at infinity, has trivial stable spherical class and exactly one nondegenerate critical point in each fiber must have zero stable tube class. This is the obstruction that the transported generating family will violate.
- **claim-morse-sublevels** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-morse-sublevels)): Suppose a family satisfying <a href="#eq:local-quadratic" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-quadratic">[eq:local-quadratic]</a> has one nondegenerate critical point and smooth homogeneous data of type $T_{k,l}$ at infinity, where $k,l\geq3$. Its Morse index is $k$. Moreover, the unit sphere bundle of its negative eigenspaces is fiber homotopy equivalent to the family of negative domains at infinity.
  - Explanation: With a single nondegenerate critical point and homogeneous data of type T_{k,l} at infinity, the Morse index equals k. The unit sphere bundle of the negative eigenspaces is fiber homotopy equivalent to the family of negative domains at infinity.
- **claim-stabilization-smoothing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-stabilization-smoothing)): Let $g_b(w)$ be a smooth homogeneous family with $\lVert\nabla g_b(w)\rVert\geq c\lVert w\rVert$, and let $q'(z)$ be a fixed nondegenerate quadratic form. Put $W=(w,z)$ and $G_0(b,W)=g_b(w)+q'(z)$. Suppose that $F_b(W)$ is smooth and that $$\lvert F_b-G_0\rvert\leq M_0,\qquad \lVert d_WF_b-d_WG_0\rVert\leq M_1$$ uniformly. Then, outside an arbitrarily large fixed ball, $F$ can be modified to agree exactly with a smooth homogeneous family $G_\eta$. The modification creates no new critical points, preserves a neighborhood of every original critical point, and the homogeneous family $G_\eta$ is homotopic through regular $C^1$ homogeneous functions to $G_0$.* *The construction is uniform over compact parameter spaces, or over any parameter domain on which the bounds used here are uniform.
  - Explanation: A family that is uniformly close, in value and vertical derivative, to a homogeneous sum with a nondegenerate quadratic form can be modified outside a large ball to agree exactly with a smooth homogeneous family. No new critical points appear and the homogeneous data stay in the same regular homotopy class.
- **claim-signed-flows** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-signed-flows)): On $$P_{\pm,b}=\{(u,t):\ \pm s_b(u,t)\geq\epsilon\}$$ there are smooth fields $V_{\pm,b}$, depending smoothly on $b$, such that $$\begin{aligned} \pm df_b(V_{\pm,b})&>0 &&\text{on }P_{\pm,b},\label{eq:field-f}\\ dt(V_{\pm,b})&>0 &&\text{near }t=0,1,\label{eq:field-t}\\ \pm ds_b(V_{\pm,b})&>0 &&\text{on }s_b=\pm\epsilon.\label{eq:field-s} \end{aligned}$$ The fields can be chosen with a common prescription extending across the whole angular sphere on each end collar.
  - Explanation: On the two complementary regions of the zero-level band there are smooth fields, depending smoothly on the parameter, along which the function increases or decreases strictly. They also move forward in the annulus coordinate near both ends and cross the band sides transversally.
- **claim-residual-hcob** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-residual-hcob)): The negative field gives an embedding $$j_b:T_-^0\hookrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{int}}T_{-,b}^1$$ which is a homotopy equivalence. It also identifies $C_b$ with $$T_{-,b}^1\setminus\mathop{\mathrm{int}}j_b(T_-^0).$$ In particular, the family $C_b$, with its fixed incoming identification with $\Sigma$, is a smooth family of h-cobordisms.
  - Explanation: Flowing along the negative field embeds the inner truncated negative tube into the outer one as a homotopy equivalence. The remaining piece is the zero-level band, so the band family is a smooth family of h-cobordisms with fixed incoming identification.
- **claim-band-is-stabilization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-band-is-stabilization)): The family $W_b$ represents the extension to $Y$ of the interval stabilization of $C_b$.
  - Explanation: The family obtained by adding trivial collars to the band is the extension to the ambient sphere of the interval stabilization of the band cobordism family.
- **claim-product-complement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-product-complement)): The complement of $W_b$ up to $Y\times\{2\}$ is, after compatible corner rounding, a product cylinder over the outgoing boundary of $W_b$, smoothly in $b$. Consequently $[W]=0$ in $[B,H(Y)]$.
  - Explanation: After compatible corner rounding, the complement of the thickened band up to the upper collar is a product cylinder over its outgoing boundary, smoothly in the parameter. Hence the class of the extended family vanishes in the h-cobordism space of the sphere.
- **claim-hcob-injectivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-hcob-injectivity)): Let $k,l\geq20$, and put $$Y=S^{k+l-1},\qquad \Sigma=\{q_{k,l}=0\}\cap Y\cong S^{k-1}\times S^{l-1}.$$ For a closed collar embedding $e:\Sigma\times J\hookrightarrow Y$, the composite $$\label{eq:hcob-injectivity-map} H(\Sigma)\xrightarrow{\sigma}H(\Sigma\times J) \xrightarrow{e_!}H(Y)\longrightarrow H^s(Y)$$ induces an isomorphism on $\pi_9$ in the product component. Consequently, if a sphere family in $H(\Sigma)$ has null image under this composite, the family is itself null.
  - Explanation: In high dimensions, interval stabilization, extension and passage to the stable h-cobordism space induce an isomorphism on the ninth homotopy group in the product component. A sphere family in H(Sigma) with null image is therefore null.
- **claim-generating-map** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-generating-map)): For a Morse family, $\Gamma_F:\operatorname{Crit}(F)\to T^*M$ is an exact Lagrangian immersion, with primitive $F\|_{\operatorname{Crit}(F)}$.
  - Explanation: For a Morse family the map sending a critical point to its base point and fiber derivative is an exact Lagrangian immersion of the critical locus into the cotangent bundle, with the restriction of the function as primitive.
- **claim-embedded-realization** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-embedded-realization)): On $Q=B\times S^{N-1}$ there is a smooth Morse family $F:Q\times\mathbb R^N\to\mathbb R$ which equals $g_b(w)$ outside a uniform vertical ball and whose generating map is a diffeomorphism from its critical locus onto a closed exact embedded Lagrangian $L\subset T^*Q$. Its critical locus, and hence $L$, is diffeomorphic to $Q$. The infinity class on each slice $B\times\{v_0\}$ is the chosen class $a$.
  - Explanation: Coupling the homogeneous family linearly to the sphere factor with a scale varying over S^9 gives a Morse family whose generating map is injective. Its image is a closed exact embedded Lagrangian diffeomorphic to Q, and the infinity class on each slice is the chosen class a.
- **claim-transport-cylinder-extension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transport-cylinder-extension)): Suppose that a smooth compactly supported Hamiltonian isotopy of $T^*Q$ sends $Q_0$ to $L$. There is a smooth Hamiltonian on $T^*\mathbb R^d$, supported for all times in one compact subset of $T^*U$, whose flow $\widetilde\varphi_t$ has the following properties. Every trajectory starting on $\mathcal L_0$ has constant radial position $\alpha$. For some $\nu_c>0$, the final image over $U_c=\iota(Q\times(-\nu_c,\nu_c)^2)$ is precisely the zero section: $$\widetilde\varphi_1(\mathcal L_0)\cap T^*U_c=(U_c)_0. \label{eq:transport-central-zero}$$
  - Explanation: A hypothetical Hamiltonian isotopy carrying the zero section to L is extended to a compactly supported Hamiltonian on Euclidean space that preserves the radial coordinates along the cylinder over L. Over a central slab the final image is exactly the zero section.
- **claim-transport-mixed-generator** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transport-mixed-generator)): For a sufficiently small increment, write $\psi(x,p)=(X,P)$ in the Euclidean cotangent coordinates. The pair $(X,p)$ is a global coordinate system on its graph. There is a smooth compactly supported function $\sigma(X,p)$ such that $$x=X+\sigma_p(X,p),\qquad P=p+\sigma_X(X,p). \label{eq:transport-mixed-graph}$$ One can choose a single compact $D\Subset U$ containing the spatial support projection of all these functions, independently of a subsequent refinement of the subdivision. Also $I+\sigma_{pX}$ is invertible, and refinement makes $\lVert\sigma_p\rVert_\infty$ arbitrarily small.
  - Explanation: A sufficiently small time increment of the flow has a compactly supported generating function in mixed position and momentum coordinates, built from the action integral. The associated matrix is invertible and the displacement tends to zero as the increment shrinks.
- **claim-transport-step** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-transport-step)): Suppose $F_o:U\times\mathbb R^r\to\mathbb R$ is a smooth Morse family, with $\Gamma_{F_o}$ a diffeomorphism onto its generated Lagrangian $\mathcal L_o\subset T^*U$. Suppose that it is smooth up to $\overline U$, with uniform bounds on compact vertical sets, and equals a homogeneous function $g_o(x,w)$ for $\lVert w\rVert\ge R_o$, where $g_o$ is smooth for $w\ne0$ and $$\lVert\nabla_wg_o(x,w)\rVert\ge c_o\lVert w\rVert, \qquad \lVert d_xg_o(x,w)\rVert\le C_o\lVert w\rVert^2, \qquad c_o>0. \label{eq:transport-old-bounds}$$ For a small step $\psi$ as in Lemma <a href="#lem:transport-mixed-generator" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:transport-mixed-generator">16</a>, there is a smooth Morse family on $U\times\mathbb R^{r+2d}$ generating $\psi(\mathcal L_o)$ with the same properties. Its critical locus corresponds diffeomorphically to $\operatorname{Crit}(F_o)$. On every fixed compact parameter slice in $U$, its homogeneous data represent the split quadratic stabilization of the preceding tube class.
  - Explanation: A Morse family generating a Lagrangian with controlled homogeneous data at infinity can be transported through one small step. The new family has the same critical locus, enlarged by split quadratic variables, and its homogeneous data represent the split stabilization of the previous tube class.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-tube-fibration",
        "exact_quote": "For the stable smooth tube space <a href=\"#eq:stable-tubes\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:stable-tubes\">[eq:stable-tubes]</a> there is a homotopy fiber sequence $$\\label{eq:tube-fibration}\n H^s(*)\\longrightarrow\\mathbf T\\xrightarrow{\\gamma}BG.$$ The rigid tube map $\\iota:BO\\to\\mathbf T$, defined by nondegenerate quadratic forms, satisfies $\\gamma\\circ\\iota\\simeq BJ$, where $BJ:BO\\to BG$ is the stable spherical fibration map.",
        "source_id": "PM-340.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-tube-fibration",
          "exact_quote": "For the stable smooth tube space <a href=\"#eq:stable-tubes\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:stable-tubes\">[eq:stable-tubes]</a> there is a homotopy fiber sequence $$\\label{eq:tube-fibration}\n H^s(*)\\longrightarrow\\mathbf T\\xrightarrow{\\gamma}BG.$$ The rigid tube map $\\iota:BO\\to\\mathbf T$, defined by nondegenerate quadratic forms, satisfies $\\gamma\\circ\\iota\\simeq BJ$, where $BJ:BO\\to BG$ is the stable spherical fibration map.",
          "source_id": "PM-340.1"
        }

      "explanation": "Waldhausen's tube fibration: the stable smooth tube space maps to BG by the negative-domain spherical fibration, and the homotopy fiber of this map is the stable h-cobordism space of a point.       "explanation": "The ninth homotopy group of the stable tube space contains a nonzero class whose image in BG vanishes. It comes from the boundary map in the long exact sequence of the tube fibration failing to be onto. The stable J-homomorphism in this degree is injective.",

      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "eq:stable-tubes",
          "exact_quote": "The diagonal is cofinal, and we use the based mapping telescope $$\\label{eq:stable-tubes}\n \\mathbf T=\\operatorname*{hocolim}_{m}\n \\bigl(T_{m,m}\\xrightarrow{\\,\\oplus q_{1,1}\\,}T_{m+1,m+1}\\bigr).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-340.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-7",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be the stable space of homotopy self-equivalences of spheres, stabilized by suspension; $BG$ classifies stable spherical fibrations.",

        },
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-7",
          "exact_quote": "The negative domains of a tube family have the homotopy type of spheres, and their stable fiber homotopy class defines a map $\\gamma:\\mathbf T\\to BG$.",

        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-one-critical",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B=S^9$ and let $p\\geq20$. Suppose that $f_b:\\mathbb R^{2p}\\to\\mathbb R$, $b\\in B$, is a smooth family satisfying the following conditions:*\n\n1.  *it is homogeneous at infinity, with smooth homogeneous data in $T_{p,p}$;*\n\n2.  *each $f_b$ has exactly one critical point, which is nondegenerate and varies smoothly with $b$;*\n\n3.  *the stable spherical class of the family at infinity is zero in $[B,BG]$.*\n\n*Then its stable tube class is zero in $[B,\\mathbf T]$.",
        "source_id": "PM-340.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-stabilization-smoothing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $g_b(w)$ be a smooth homogeneous family with $\\lVert\\nabla g_b(w)\\rVert\\geq c\\lVert w\\rVert$, and let $q'(z)$ be a fixed nondegenerate quadratic form. Put $W=(w,z)$ and $G_0(b,W)=g_b(w)+q'(z)$. Suppose that $F_b(W)$ is smooth and that $$\\lvert F_b-G_0\\rvert\\leq M_0,\\qquad\n \\lVert d_WF_b-d_WG_0\\rVert\\leq M_1$$ uniformly. Then, outside an arbitrarily large fixed ball, $F$ can be modified to agree exactly with a smooth homogeneous family $G_\\eta$. The modification creates no new critical points, preserves a neighborhood of every original critical point, and the homogeneous family $G_\\eta$ is homotopic through regular $C^1$ homogeneous functions to $G_0$.*\n\n*The construction is uniform over compact parameter spaces, or over any parameter domain on which the bounds used here are uniform.",

<!-- PM:END pm.claims.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Lagrangian and exact Lagrangian submanifold** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A smooth submanifold $L\subset T^*Q$ is Lagrangian if it has dimension $\dim Q$ and $d\lambda$ vanishes on it; it is exact if $\lambda\|_L=df$ for a globally defined function $f:L\to\mathbb R$.
- **Hamiltonian isotopy** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A Hamiltonian isotopy has a smooth time-dependent Hamiltonian supported in one fixed compact subset of $T^*Q$.
- **Quadratic form q_{k,l}** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For positive integers $k,l$, write $$q_{k,l}(x,y)=-\lVert x\rVert^{2}+\lVert y\rVert^{2}, \qquad (x,y)\in\mathbb R^k\oplus\mathbb R^l.$$
- **Tube space T_{k,l}** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Let $T_{k,l}$ be the path component of $q_{k,l}\|_{S^{k+l-1}}$ in $$\{h\in C^1(S^{k+l-1},\mathbb R):0\text{ is a regular value of }h\},$$ with its $C^1$ topology.
- **Negative domain D(h) and smooth tube** ([source](#pm-h-6)): When $h$ is smooth, $g$ is smooth off the origin, and its negative domain $$D(h)=\{u\in S^{k+l-1}:h(u)\leq0\}$$ is a smooth tube: a domain isotopic to $D(q_{k,l})\cong S^{k-1}\times D^l$.
- **Direct sum of homogeneous functions** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Direct sum is well defined in this model: $$(g\oplus g')(w,z)=g(w)+g'(z).$$
- **Stable smooth tube space T** ([source](#pm-h-6)): The diagonal is cofinal, and we use the based mapping telescope $$\label{eq:stable-tubes} \mathbf T=\operatorname*{hocolim}_{m} \bigl(T_{m,m}\xrightarrow{\,\oplus q_{1,1}\,}T_{m+1,m+1}\bigr).$$
- **Group G and classifying space BG** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Let $G$ be the stable space of homotopy self-equivalences of spheres, stabilized by suspension; $BG$ classifies stable spherical fibrations.
- **Spherical-fibration map gamma** ([source](#pm-h-7)): The negative domains of a tube family have the homotopy type of spheres, and their stable fiber homotopy class defines a map $\gamma:\mathbf T\to BG$.
- **Stable h-cobordism space H^s(X)** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Its stable version is $$H^s(X)=\operatorname*{hocolim}_{r}H(X\times J^r),$$ where $J$ is a closed interval and the maps are interval product stabilizations.
- **Smooth Whitehead space** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Here $\operatorname{Wh}^{\mathrm{diff}}(X)$ denotes the infinite loop space of the cofiber of the unit $\Sigma^\infty X_+\to A(X)$, where $A(X)$ is Waldhausen’s algebraic $K$-theory spectrum of $X$.
- **Homogeneous at infinity** ([source](#sec:morse)): A family is *homogeneous at infinity* if it agrees, outside one fixed ball in every fiber, with a family of degree-two homogeneous functions.
- **Interval stabilization** ([source](#pm-h-13)): The interval stabilization used here is $\sigma:H(X)\to H(X\times J)$, where $J$ is a closed interval.
- **Extension of h-cobordisms** ([source](#pm-h-13)): If $e:X\hookrightarrow Y$ is a codimension-zero embedding, extension $e_!:H(X)\to H(Y)$ glues the cobordism to the trivial cylinder on $Y\setminus\mathop{\mathrm{int}}e(X)$ along the product vertical boundary.
- **Boundary sphere Y and corner manifold Sigma** ([source](#sec:obstruction)): Put $$Y=S^{k+l-1},\qquad \Sigma=\{q_{k,l}=0\}\cap Y\cong S^{k-1}\times S^{l-1}.$$
- **Rescaled function s_b** ([source](#sec:obstruction)): Write $$s_b(u,t)=\frac{f_b(r(t)u)}{r(t)^2}.$$
- **Morse family** ([source](#sec:embedding)): If $M$ is a smooth manifold, a smooth function $F:M\times\mathbb R^r\to\mathbb R$ is a *Morse family* when zero is a regular value of the full map $d_wF:M\times\mathbb R^r\to(\mathbb R^r)^*$.
- **Critical locus and generating map** ([source](#sec:embedding)): Write $$\operatorname{Crit}(F)=\{(q,w):d_wF(q,w)=0\},\qquad \Gamma_F(q,w)=(q,d_qF(q,w)).$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-fiber-index-lower-bound | fiber index p in the one-critical-point obstruction | ≥ | $20$ | p at least twenty. |
| q-base-sphere-dimension | dimension of the parameter sphere B | = | $9$ | Fixed by the choice of the homotopy group degree. |
| q-boundary-image-bound | order of the image of the boundary map | ≤ | $4$ | Boundary from the tenth homotopy group of BG. |
| q-gradient-two-sided | vertical gradient of the homogeneous extension | ≤ | $c\lVert w\rVert\leq\lVert\nabla_wg_b(w)\rVert\leq C\lVert w\rVert$ | All b in B and all w; constants c and C positive and finite. |
| q-mixed-derivative-bound | mixed derivative of the homogeneous family | ≤ | $\lVert d_wd_bg_b(w)\rVert\leq C_1\lVert w\rVert$ | Constant C_1 from compactness. |
| q-morse-index-threshold | indices k and l in the Morse sublevel lemma | ≥ | $3$ | Both indices at least three. |
| q-unbalanced-index | positive index l after stabilization | > | $k+9$ | k equals p. |
| q-igusa-range | dimension of X for r-connected interval stabilization | ≥ | $\max(2r+8,3r+5)$ | Connectivity r. |
| q-igusa-threshold-ten | stability bound for connectivity ten | = | $35$ | r equals ten. |
| q-hcob-index-bound | indices k and l in the h-cobordism injectivity statement | ≥ | $20$ | Both indices at least twenty. |
| q-sigma-dimension | dimension of Sigma | ≥ | $38$ | Both sphere indices at least twenty. |
| q-cobordism-dimension | dimension of the h-cobordism | ≥ | $39$ | Cobordisms on Sigma. |
| q-scale-separation | scale constant K against the height-function gradient | > | $K\delta_0>C_1/c$ | Chosen first in the order K, Lambda, T. |
| q-scale-function | scale function R(b) | = | $\Lambda e^{K\theta(b)}$ | Lambda chosen large after K. |
| q-ambient-dimension | dimension d of the ambient Euclidean space | = | $10+N$ | N even. |
| q-final-fiber-index | final fiber half-dimension p after the transport steps | = | $m+ds$ | s increments of the subdivision. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-tube-fibration** [claim](#stmt-thm-tube-fibration) / [proof](#stmt-thm-tube-fibration): Cite Waldhausen's smooth tube theorem, with comparison of function and domain models and the rigid map. -> homotopy fiber sequence from the stable h-cobordism space of a point to T to BG
- **step-kernel-class** [claim](#stmt-prop-kernel-class) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-kernel-class): Long exact sequence, order counting of the image of the boundary map against the Whitehead group, and injectivity of the J-homomorphism. -> nonzero class in the ninth homotopy group of T killed by gamma
- **step-localized-family** [claim](#stmt-lem-localized-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-localized-family): Finite-stage representative, degree-one collapse, relative angular smoothing and compactness bounds. -> smooth family with standard form near the critical points and uniform gradient bounds
- **step-stabilization-smoothing** [claim](#stmt-lem-stabilization-smoothing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stabilization-smoothing): Angular cutoff of the homogeneous term, then radial interpolation with parameters chosen in order. -> exactly homogeneous smooth data outside a ball without new critical points
- **step-morse-sublevels** [claim](#stmt-lem-morse-sublevels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-morse-sublevels): Descending field on noncompact sublevels, local handle model and the homological Whitehead theorem. -> index equals k and negative eigenspace sphere bundle matches negative domains at infinity
- **step-signed-flows** [claim](#stmt-lem-signed-flows) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-signed-flows): Explicit field on the end collars and signed gradient flow elsewhere, patched convexly. -> smooth transverse fields on the two complementary regions
- **step-residual-hcob** [claim](#stmt-lem-residual-hcob) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-residual-hcob): Flow decomposition, excision, van Kampen and Poincare–Lefschetz duality. -> smooth family of h-cobordisms with fixed incoming identification
- **step-band-stabilization** [claim](#stmt-lem-band-is-stabilization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-band-is-stabilization): Compare the collar construction with the faces of the interval stabilization, also on transition maps. -> band family equals extended interval stabilization
- **step-product-complement** [claim](#stmt-lem-product-complement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-product-complement): Transverse field with corner rounding, finite hitting times and a collar compression. -> product complement and vanishing class in the h-cobordism space of Y
- **step-hcob-injectivity** [claim](#stmt-prop-hcob-injectivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-hcob-injectivity): Igusa stability range and connectivity of extension, then orbit argument for free classes. -> isomorphism on the ninth homotopy group detecting nullity
- **step-one-critical** [claim](#stmt-prop-one-critical) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-hcob-injectivity-2): Normalize, unbalance indices, remove the cobordism, straighten the cores by multijet transversality. -> stable tube class vanishes
- **step-generating-map** [claim](#stmt-lem-generating-map) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-generating-map): Surjectivity of the critical differential gives an immersion; pullback of the canonical form gives exactness. -> exact Lagrangian immersion
- **step-embedded-realization** [claim](#stmt-prop-embedded-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-embedded-realization): Linear coupling with a varying scale, sphere momenta then base momenta to prove injectivity, compactness for embedding. -> closed exact embedded Lagrangian diffeomorphic to Q
- **step-transport-cylinder** [claim](#stmt-lem-transport-cylinder-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transport-cylinder-extension): Reverse the isotopy and extend it with cutoffs so that radial coordinates stay constant. -> compactly supported Euclidean flow whose image over a central slab is the zero section
- **step-mixed-generator** [claim](#stmt-lem-transport-mixed-generator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transport-mixed-generator): Invert the position map and build the primitive from the action integral. -> mixed generating function with controlled support
- **step-transport-step** [claim](#stmt-prop-transport-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-transport-step): Shifted family with cutoffs, regularity of the critical equations, correspondence of critical loci, and smoothing at infinity. -> Morse family generating the transported Lagrangian with stabilized tube class
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-transport-step-2): Assume an isotopy, transport the family through all steps, restrict to a slice and contradict the one-critical-point obstruction. -> no Hamiltonian isotopy carries the zero section to L

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-main-nonisotopic-lagrangian**: For a suitable large even N, the cotangent bundle of S^9 x S^(N-1) contains a closed exact embedded Lagrangian diffeomorphic to the base that no compactly supported Hamiltonian isotopy carries from the zero section.
- **inv-kernel-class-injective-j**: A nonzero class in the ninth homotopy group of the stable tube space has trivial image in the stable spherical fibrations, and the J-map on the ninth homotopy group of BO is injective.
- **inv-one-critical-point-obstruction**: A smooth family over S^9 that is homogeneous at infinity, has trivial stable spherical class and exactly one nondegenerate critical point per fiber must have zero stable tube class.
- **inv-embedded-realization**: A Morse family built from the homogeneous family and a scaled linear coupling to a sphere factor has a generating map that is a diffeomorphism from its critical locus onto a closed exact embedded Lagrangian diffeomorphic to the base.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-low-dimension-claims**: Cite the counterexample to claim exotic nearby Lagrangians in low-dimensional cotangent bundles. -> Positive isotopy results are known in low dimension, and the construction needs a high-dimensional base with a sphere factor and large fiber indices.
- **anti-projection-topology**: Use the result to conclude that the cotangent projection restricted to L is not a homotopy equivalence. -> The obstruction concerns the ambient isotopy class only; the projection topology is a separate matter.
- **anti-other-isotopy-classes**: Extend the conclusion to Hamiltonian isotopies that are not compactly supported or to other base manifolds. -> The proof uses compact support throughout transport and the specific base with a sphere factor.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are existence and non-existence statements in symplectic and homotopy theory, proved by homotopy-group exactness, h-cobordism stability and a contradiction argument; they give no finite procedure over finite inputs, so no executable reference or oracle can decide them.

It has no stated algorithm with cost, termination or arithmetic model, and the key class is obtained non-constructively from homotopy-group exactness. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The tube fibration is a homotopy fiber sequence of spaces. It organises topological types and carries no representation, indexing or update operations that a data structure could implement. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Morse-index and sublevel-set arguments on noncompact fibers are purely topological. There is no dataflow, kernel or arithmetic workload to map onto hardware. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper involves hardness assumptions, adversaries or keys. The smoothing lemma concerns removing critical points of homogeneous functions, which has no security meaning. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The families of functions with one critical point are smooth parametrised objects over a sphere, not learned models. No loss landscape, training procedure or generalisation statement is made; the result is an obstruction on critical-point structure for a specific class of families. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Gradient-like fields are used only to build flows that exhibit an h-cobordism between level sets. No objective is optimised, and no convergence or optimality claim is made. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There are no processes, messages or consistency requirements. The mixed generating function is a local description of a symplectic map for a small time increment. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The natural home of the result is symplectic and differential topology; it is a counterexample to a conjecture with no engineering construction attached, so no further engineering domain is proposed. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-even-large-N** (hypothesis): The main counterexample is asserted only for some sufficiently large even integer N, with base S^9 times S^(N-1). The paper does not give the value of N or say which N work. Blocked use: ['Applying the counterexample to a specific named N without a separate argument', 'Applying it to odd N or to other base manifolds']
- **limit-compact-support-isotopy** (formal_scope): The non-isotopy conclusion concerns compactly supported Hamiltonian isotopies carrying the zero section to L. Other isotopy notions are not covered. Blocked use: ['Claiming non-isotopy for isotopies that are not compactly supported', 'Reading the theorem as a statement about the cotangent projection']
- **limit-fiber-index-twenty** (hypothesis): The obstruction for families with one critical point needs base S^9 and fiber index p at least twenty. Smaller fiber dimensions are not covered by the argument. Blocked use: ['Applying the obstruction to families with p below twenty', 'Applying it over a base other than S^9']
- **limit-hcob-index-twenty** (hypothesis): The injectivity of stabilization and extension on the ninth homotopy group is proved only for k and l at least twenty. The dimension bounds of the stability theorem and the smooth h-cobordism theorem are used there. Blocked use: ['Using the injectivity statement for k or l below twenty', 'Replacing the stability range by a weaker dimension bound']
- **limit-nonconstructive-kernel-class** (nonconstructive): The nonzero kernel class is obtained from exactness of a long exact sequence and an order comparison, using tabulated homotopy groups taken from the literature. No explicit representative is constructed. Blocked use: ['Presenting the class as explicitly computed or algorithmically obtainable', 'Using the construction as an executable procedure']
- **limit-contradiction-proof** (nonconstructive): Non-isotopy is proved by contradiction from a hypothetical isotopy, transported step by step through finitely many small steps whose constants are chosen anew each time. The proof gives no effective bound on the number of steps or the constants. Blocked use: ['Reading the transport as a numerical integration scheme', 'Extracting effective bounds on the subdivision']
- **limit-constant-order** (hypothesis): The embedding construction requires its constants to be chosen in the stated order, first K, then Lambda, then T. The separation condition on K depends on the gradient bounds of the family. Blocked use: ['Choosing the scale constants in another order', 'Dropping the gradient bounds when separating branches']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We disprove the unrestricted nearby Lagrangian conjecture. For some sufficiently large even integer <i>N</i>, we construct a closed exact smoothly embedded Lagrangian in $`T^*(S^9\times S^{N-1})`$ that is diffeomorphic to the base but is not Hamiltonian isotopic to the zero section.
