The parameter space in <a href="#eq:critical-parametrization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:critical-parametrization">[eq:critical-parametrization]</a> is compact and has no boundary. A compact injective immersion is an embedding, so its image is a closed exact embedded $L\cong Q$. In fact Euler’s identity gives the global primitive $F|_{\operatorname{Crit}(F)}=-g_b(w)$, although the exactness argument did not require this formula. Finally, for $\lVert w\rVert\geq2T$, the family equals $g_b(w)$ independently of $v$. Its restriction at infinity to any fixed sphere slice therefore represents $a$. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:transport"></a>
### Hamiltonian transport and the obstruction

We now transport the generating family of Proposition <a href="#prop:embedded-realization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:embedded-realization">14</a> under a hypothetical Hamiltonian isotopy. The construction below keeps track of its actual homogeneous data at infinity. Each step adds a fixed quadratic form of split signature, followed by a homotopy through regular homogeneous data. In particular, the construction applies to the nonconstant tube family used here. The construction follows the classical strategy of composing with small Hamiltonian steps and adding split quadratic variables (Sikorav 1987, secs. 1.3–1.7) <a id="pm-cite-46"></a>[[Sikorav1987](#ref-Sikorav1987)]; generating families and their invariance under isotopy are also developed in (Eliashberg and Gromov 1998) <a id="pm-cite-47"></a>[[EG1998](#ref-EG1998)]. For a related generating-family lifting statement, see Álvarez-Gavela et al. (Álvarez-Gavela, Igusa, and Sullivan 2026, Lemma 3.23) <a id="pm-cite-48"></a>[[AIS2026](#ref-AIS2026)]; the argument needed here is given directly.

We use the Morse families and generating maps $\Gamma_F$ of Section <a href="#sec:embedding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:embedding">5</a>. For any manifold $M$, write $M_0\subset T^*M$ for its zero section. Every family constructed below has $\Gamma_F$ a diffeomorphism from $\operatorname{Crit}(F)$ onto the Lagrangian it generates.

<a id="pm-h-18"></a>
#### A Euclidean cylinder and a compactly supported extension

<a id="eq:transport-initial-family"></a>
Retain $N=2m$ and $Q=S^9\times S^{N-1}$ from the preceding construction, and put $d=10+N$. View $Q$ as the product of unit spheres in $\mathbb R^{10}\times\mathbb R^N=\mathbb R^d$. For $0<\nu<1$, the map $$\iota:Q\times(-\nu,\nu)^2\longrightarrow\mathbb R^d,
 \qquad
 \iota((b,v),\alpha)=((1+\alpha_1)b,(1+\alpha_2)v)$$ is a diffeomorphism onto a bounded open neighborhood $U$ of $Q$. Its inverse, the projection $r_Q:U\to Q$, and its two radial coordinates extend smoothly to a neighborhood of $\overline U$. The cotangent lift gives the canonical identification $$T^*U\cong T^*Q\times T^*((-\nu,\nu)^2),
 \qquad
 \lambda_U=\lambda_Q+p_\alpha\cdot d\alpha.$$ If $F$ is the family of Proposition <a href="#prop:embedded-realization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:embedded-realization">14</a>, then $$F^{(0)}(x,w)=F(r_Q(x),w)
 \label{eq:transport-initial-family}$$ is a Morse family whose generated image is the cylinder <a id="eq:transport-cylinder"></a>$$\mathcal L_0=
 \{(q,\alpha;p_q,0):(q,p_q)\in L,\ \alpha\in(-\nu,\nu)^2\}.
 \label{eq:transport-cylinder}$$ Its critical parametrization is a diffeomorphism. All its vertical critical points lie in a uniform ball, and it has the stated smoothness and homogeneous estimates uniformly up to $\overline U$.

<a id="stmt-lem-transport-cylinder-extension"></a>
<div id="lem:transport-cylinder-extension" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:transport-central-zero"></a>
**Lemma 15** (Extension of the reverse isotopy). *Suppose that a smooth compactly supported Hamiltonian isotopy of $T^*Q$ sends $Q_0$ to $L$. There is a smooth Hamiltonian on $T^*\mathbb R^d$, supported for all times in one compact subset of $T^*U$, whose flow $\widetilde\varphi_t$ has the following properties. Every trajectory starting on $\mathcal L_0$ has constant radial position $\alpha$. For some $\nu_c>0$, the final image over $U_c=\iota(Q\times(-\nu_c,\nu_c)^2)$ is precisely the zero section: $$\widetilde\varphi_1(\mathcal L_0)\cap T^*U_c=(U_c)_0.
 \label{eq:transport-central-zero}$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-transport-cylinder-extension"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-transport-cylinder-extension</summary>

*Proof.* Let $\varphi_t$ be the assumed original flow, generated by $H_t$. The path $\varphi_{1-t}\circ\varphi_1^{-1}$ starts at the identity, ends at $\varphi_1^{-1}$, and is generated by <a id="eq:transport-reverse-sign"></a>$$H_t^Q=-H_{1-t}.
 \label{eq:transport-reverse-sign}$$ Indeed differentiation of this path gives $-X_{H_{1-t}}$ at its current point; the fixed composition on the right introduces no conjugation of the Hamiltonian.

Choose $0\le\zeta\le1$ supported in $(-\nu,\nu)^2$ and identically $1$ on $(-\nu_c,\nu_c)^2$. Choose $0\le\xi\le1$ of compact support in $\mathbb R^2$, with $\xi=1$ on a ball to be specified. On $T^*U$ set <a id="eq:transport-extended-hamiltonian"></a>$$\widetilde H_t(q,\alpha;p_q,p_\alpha)
   =H_t^Q(q,p_q)\zeta(\alpha)\xi(p_\alpha),
 \label{eq:transport-extended-hamiltonian}$$ and extend by zero to $T^*\mathbb R^d$. This is smooth with uniform compact support: $Q$ is compact, $H_t^Q$ has uniformly compact momentum support, and both additional coordinate pairs are cut off.

The convention $\iota_{X_H}d\lambda=-dH$ gives the radial equations <a id="eq:transport-radial-equations"></a>$$\dot\alpha=H_t^Q\zeta\,\nabla\xi(p_\alpha),
 \qquad
 \dot p_\alpha=-H_t^Q\nabla\zeta\,\xi(p_\alpha).
 \label{eq:transport-radial-equations}$$ Set $$M=\sup_{t,q,p_q}\lvert H_t^Q(q,p_q)\rvert\,\sup_\alpha\lVert\nabla\zeta(\alpha)\rVert.$$ This number is independent of the size of the plateau of $\xi$. Take $\xi=1$ on $\{\lVert p_\alpha\rVert\le M+1\}$. A trajectory starting on $\mathcal L_0$ starts with $p_\alpha=0$, and the second equation gives $\lVert p_\alpha(t)\rVert\le Mt$ for $0\le t\le1$. Hence it stays in this plateau, the first equation vanishes, and $\alpha$ is constant. When $\alpha\in(-\nu_c,\nu_c)^2$, also $\nabla\zeta=0$; then $p_\alpha=0$ and the $T^*Q$ motion is exactly the reverse isotopy. It sends $L$ to $Q_0$. Since all radial positions on the entire cylinder are fixed, no other part of that cylinder enters the central slab. This proves <a href="#eq:transport-central-zero" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-central-zero">[eq:transport-central-zero]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-19"></a>
#### A compact mixed generating function for each small step

Fix a compact subset $K\Subset T^*U$ containing the support of $\widetilde H_t$ in its interior for every $t$. Points outside $K$ are stationary, and uniqueness of flow lines prevents a trajectory from crossing into the stationary exterior in finite time. In particular, all the flow maps and their successive time increments are supported in this common compact region. Smoothness in time and compactness allow a finite subdivision $$0=t_0<t_1<\cdots<t_s=1$$ such that every map $\psi_j=\widetilde\varphi_{t_j}\widetilde\varphi_{t_{j-1}}^{-1}$ is as close to the identity in $C^1$ as required below.

<a id="stmt-lem-transport-mixed-generator"></a>
<div id="lem:transport-mixed-generator" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:transport-mixed-graph"></a>
**Lemma 16** (Mixed coordinates and the action primitive). *For a sufficiently small increment, write $\psi(x,p)=(X,P)$ in the Euclidean cotangent coordinates. The pair $(X,p)$ is a global coordinate system on its graph. There is a smooth compactly supported function $\sigma(X,p)$ such that $$x=X+\sigma_p(X,p),\qquad P=p+\sigma_X(X,p).
 \label{eq:transport-mixed-graph}$$ One can choose a single compact $D\Subset U$ containing the spatial support projection of all these functions, independently of a subsequent refinement of the subdivision. Also $I+\sigma_{pX}$ is invertible, and refinement makes $\lVert\sigma_p\rVert_\infty$ arbitrarily small.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-transport-mixed-generator"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-transport-mixed-generator</summary>

*Proof.* Require $\lVert D_xX-I\rVert<\varepsilon<1$ uniformly. For each fixed $p$, the map $x\mapsto X(x,p)$ is a local diffeomorphism and satisfies $$\lVert X(x_1,p)-X(x_2,p)\rVert\ge(1-\varepsilon)\lVert x_1-x_2\rVert.$$ It is injective and proper, since it is the identity outside a compact set. Its image is both open and closed in $\mathbb R^d$, so it is onto. The inverse $x=x(X,p)$ is smooth jointly in $(X,p)$.

For a step from time $a$ to time $b$, let $\varphi_{s,a}$ denote its partial flow. Cartan’s formula and the sign convention give $$\mathcal L_{X_{\widetilde H_s}}\lambda
   =d\bigl(\lambda(X_{\widetilde H_s})-\widetilde H_s\bigr).$$ Consequently the action integral <a id="eq:transport-action"></a>$$A(x,p)=\int_a^b
  \bigl(\lambda(X_{\widetilde H_s})-\widetilde H_s\bigr)
          (\varphi_{s,a}(x,p))\,ds
 \label{eq:transport-action}$$ satisfies $\psi^*\lambda-\lambda=dA$. It is compactly supported: outside $K$ the trajectory is stationary and its integrand is zero. On the graph, expressed in mixed coordinates, put $$S(X,p)=A(x(X,p),p)+x(X,p)\cdot p.$$ Then $$dS=P\cdot dX+x\cdot dp.$$ Writing $S=X\cdot p+\sigma$ gives <a href="#eq:transport-mixed-graph" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-mixed-graph">[eq:transport-mixed-graph]</a>. Explicitly, <a id="eq:transport-compact-sigma"></a>$$\sigma(X,p)=A(x(X,p),p)+(x(X,p)-X)\cdot p.
 \label{eq:transport-compact-sigma}$$ This expression vanishes outside the compact mixed-coordinate image of $K$. Its spatial projection lies in a fixed compact subset of $U$, because both the input and output trajectories remain in $K$. Enlarge that projection slightly to obtain $D\Subset U$. Thus for every $X\in U\setminus D$, $\sigma$ and all its derivatives vanish on a neighborhood of $X$, for all momenta. Finally $I+\sigma_{pX}=D_Xx$ is invertible, and $\sigma_p=x-X$ tends uniformly to zero with the length of the time increment. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-20"></a>
#### Transport of one Morse family

The following step supplies the analytic induction. The variables called $w$ include all the stabilizing variables introduced at earlier steps.

<a id="stmt-prop-transport-step"></a>
<div id="prop:transport-step" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:transport-old-bounds"></a>
**Proposition 17** (One transport step with controlled infinity data). *Suppose $F_o:U\times\mathbb R^r\to\mathbb R$ is a smooth Morse family, with $\Gamma_{F_o}$ a diffeomorphism onto its generated Lagrangian $\mathcal L_o\subset T^*U$. Suppose that it is smooth up to $\overline U$, with uniform bounds on compact vertical sets, and equals a homogeneous function $g_o(x,w)$ for $\lVert w\rVert\ge R_o$, where $g_o$ is smooth for $w\ne0$ and $$\lVert\nabla_wg_o(x,w)\rVert\ge c_o\lVert w\rVert,
 \qquad \lVert d_xg_o(x,w)\rVert\le C_o\lVert w\rVert^2,
 \qquad c_o>0.
 \label{eq:transport-old-bounds}$$ For a small step $\psi$ as in Lemma <a href="#lem:transport-mixed-generator" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:transport-mixed-generator">16</a>, there is a smooth Morse family on $U\times\mathbb R^{r+2d}$ generating $\psi(\mathcal L_o)$ with the same properties. Its critical locus corresponds diffeomorphically to $\operatorname{Crit}(F_o)$. On every fixed compact parameter slice in $U$, its homogeneous data represent the split quadratic stabilization of the preceding tube class.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-transport-step"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-transport-step</summary>

*Proof.* Introduce $z,p\in\mathbb R^d$. Before choosing cutoffs, consider the local expression $$F_o(X+z,w)+\sigma(X,p)-z\cdot p,
 \qquad X+z\in U.$$ Its $p$- and $z$-equations are $z=\sigma_p(X,p)$ and $p=\nabla_xF_o(X+z,w)$. Together with the old $w$-equation, these are precisely the mixed graph equations for composition with $\psi$: $z$ is the displacement from the new base point $X$ to the old one, and $p$ is the old momentum. The cutoffs below keep the shifted base point in $U$ and switch off the shift for large $\lVert w\rVert$. We will then compare the result with $g_o(X,w)-z\cdot p$ to restore homogeneity at infinity.

<a id="eq:transport-buffer"></a>
<a id="eq:transport-log-cutoff"></a>
*Choice of cutoffs.* Choose $\zeta_1:U\to[0,1]$ of compact support, identically $1$ on an open set $V$ containing $D$. Fix $\delta_0>0$ so that $$2\delta_0<\operatorname{dist}(\operatorname{supp}\zeta_1,\partial U),
 \qquad \lVert\sigma_p\rVert_\infty<\delta_0.
 \label{eq:transport-buffer}$$ The first choice is made before refining the subdivision to obtain the second. Let $\beta_1:\mathbb R^d\to[0,1]$ be $1$ for $\lVert z\rVert\le\delta_0$ and $0$ for $\lVert z\rVert\ge2\delta_0$. Choose a smooth radial $\chi:\mathbb R^r\to[0,1]$, equal to $1$ on a ball strictly larger than $\{\lVert w\rVert\le R_o\}$, of compact support, and such that $$\lVert\nabla\chi(w)\rVert\le\delta_1/\lVert w\rVert\quad(w\ne0).
 \label{eq:transport-log-cutoff}$$ Here $\delta_1>0$ may be arbitrarily small. For example, after fixing the inner radius $R_1>R_o$, take $\chi(w)=\vartheta(\log(\lVert w\rVert/R_1)/T)$ outside that radius, where $\vartheta$ is a fixed smooth cutoff equal to $1$ near $(-\infty,0]$ and $0$ near $[1,\infty)$; choosing $T$ sufficiently large gives <a href="#eq:transport-log-cutoff" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-log-cutoff">[eq:transport-log-cutoff]</a>. Extend it by $1$ at smaller radii.

Define <a id="eq:transport-kappa"></a><a id="eq:transport-shifted-family"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 \kappa(X,z,w)&=\zeta_1(X)\beta_1(z)\chi(w),
 &x&=X+\kappa(X,z,w)z,\label{eq:transport-kappa}\\
 \widehat F(X,w,z,p)
   &=F_o(X+\kappa(X,z,w)z,w)+\sigma(X,p)-z\cdot p.
 \label{eq:transport-shifted-family}
\end{aligned}$$ The shifted argument always lies in $U$. Indeed, when the shift is nonzero, $X\in\operatorname{supp}\zeta_1$ and its length is less than $2\delta_0$; the buffer in <a href="#eq:transport-buffer" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-buffer">[eq:transport-buffer]</a> places that entire ball in $U$. When the shift vanishes the assertion is immediate.

<a id="eq:transport-critical-p"></a>
<a id="eq:transport-output"></a>
*The full critical equations.* Write $a=\nabla_xF_o(x,w)$, identifying Euclidean covectors and vectors. Direct differentiation, before discarding any cutoff terms, gives <a id="eq:transport-critical-z"></a><a id="eq:transport-critical-w"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 \widehat F_p&=\sigma_p(X,p)-z,\label{eq:transport-critical-p}\\
 \widehat F_z&=\kappa a+(a\cdot z)\nabla_z\kappa-p,
       \label{eq:transport-critical-z}\\
 \widehat F_w&=\nabla_wF_o(x,w)+(a\cdot z)\nabla_w\kappa,
       \label{eq:transport-critical-w}\\
 \widehat F_X&=a+(a\cdot z)\nabla_X\kappa+\sigma_X(X,p).
       \label{eq:transport-output}
\end{aligned}$$ At any vertical critical point, <a href="#eq:transport-critical-p" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-critical-p">[eq:transport-critical-p]</a> forces $z=\sigma_p(X,p)$, hence $\lVert z\rVert<\delta_0$ and $\beta_1=1$ on a neighborhood of that point. When $\lVert w\rVert\ge R_o$, the old function is homogeneous at every allowed shifted base point. Equations <a href="#eq:transport-old-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-old-bounds">[eq:transport-old-bounds]</a> and <a href="#eq:transport-log-cutoff" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-log-cutoff">[eq:transport-log-cutoff]</a> therefore imply <a id="eq:transport-outer-gradient"></a>$$\lVert\widehat F_w\rVert
 \ge(c_o-C_o\delta_0\delta_1)\lVert w\rVert.
 \label{eq:transport-outer-gradient}$$ Choose $\delta_1$ so that $C_o\delta_0\delta_1<c_o/2$. Thus no vertical critical point has $\lVert w\rVert\ge R_o$; at every one of them $\chi=1$ on a neighborhood as well.

There are two open regions to consider, namely $V$ and $U\setminus D$; they cover $U$. For $X\in V$, the three cutoff factors are $1$ near a solution, and the critical equations become <a id="eq:transport-supported-critical"></a>$$\nabla_wF_o(x,w)=0,
 \qquad p=\nabla_xF_o(x,w),
 \qquad x=X+\sigma_p(X,p),
 \qquad x=X+z.
 \label{eq:transport-supported-critical}$$ They express exactly composition with the graph <a href="#eq:transport-mixed-graph" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-mixed-graph">[eq:transport-mixed-graph]</a>, and the generated covector is <a id="eq:transport-supported-output"></a>$$\widehat F_X=p+\sigma_X(X,p)=P.
 \label{eq:transport-supported-output}$$ The critical equation is regular here. In local total coordinates $(x,w,p,X)$, first impose the regular old equation $\nabla_wF_o=0$. With $(x,w)$ fixed, the equation $p-\nabla_xF_o=0$ has identity coefficient in $p$. With $(x,w,p)$ fixed, the last graph equation has invertible coefficient $I+\sigma_{pX}$ in $X$. This triangular order proves surjectivity of the differential of the full new vertical-gradient map.

<a id="eq:transport-outside-critical"></a>
For $X\in U\setminus D$, the function $\sigma$ vanishes on a spatial neighborhood for all $p$. The equations instead reduce to $$z=0,\qquad x=X,\qquad
 \nabla_wF_o(X,w)=0,\qquad
 p=\zeta_1(X)\nabla_xF_o(X,w).
 \label{eq:transport-outside-critical}$$ The auxiliary variable $p$ may therefore differ from the old momentum. Nevertheless, <a href="#eq:transport-output" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-output">[eq:transport-output]</a> gives exactly <a id="eq:transport-outside-output"></a>$$\widehat F_X=\nabla_xF_o(X,w),
 \label{eq:transport-outside-output}$$ because all the spatial cutoff terms are multiplied by $z=0$. This is the identity correspondence, which is the true graph of $\psi$ over this region. For regularity, first use the equation $-z=0$ to eliminate $z$, then the equation $\zeta_1\nabla_xF_o-p=0$ to eliminate $p$; the remaining equation is the regular old vertical-gradient equation. This also checks the entire transition region of $\zeta_1$. The two descriptions agree on the overlap, where $\zeta_1=1$.

<a id="eq:transport-critical-correspondence"></a>
*The correspondence of critical loci.* For an old critical point $(x,w)$ put $p_o=\nabla_xF_o(x,w)$ and $(X,P)=\psi(x,p_o)$. Define $$(x,w)\longmapsto
 \bigl(X,w,z=x-X,\ p=\zeta_1(X)p_o\bigr).
 \label{eq:transport-critical-correspondence}$$ If $X\in V$, these are the mixed graph equations, with $p=p_o$. If $X\notin D$, the true graph is the identity, so $x=X$, $z=0$, and <a href="#eq:transport-outside-critical" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-outside-critical">[eq:transport-outside-critical]</a> applies. Thus <a href="#eq:transport-critical-correspondence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-critical-correspondence">[eq:transport-critical-correspondence]</a> is a smooth map into $\operatorname{Crit}(\widehat F)$ on all of $\operatorname{Crit}(F_o)$. Its inverse is <a id="eq:transport-critical-inverse"></a>$$(X,w,z,p)\longmapsto(X+\kappa(X,z,w)z,w).
 \label{eq:transport-critical-inverse}$$ The preceding equations show that these maps are inverse and that the generated image is exactly $\psi(\mathcal L_o)$. The step preserves $T^*U$, since its support is compactly contained there. Thus no old or new critical point is lost at the base boundary, and $\Gamma_{\widehat F}$ is a diffeomorphism onto the transported image. All new solutions obey a uniform bound: $\lVert w\rVert<R_o$, $\lVert z\rVert<\delta_0$, and $$\lVert p\rVert\le
 \sup\{ \lVert\nabla_xF_o(x,w)\rVert:x\in\overline U, \lVert w\rVert\le R_o\}<\infty.$$

*Restoring exact homogeneity at infinity.* Set $W=(w,z,p)$ and consider the homogeneous reference <a id="eq:transport-homogeneous-reference"></a>$$G_0(X,W)=g_o(X,w)-z\cdot p.
 \label{eq:transport-homogeneous-reference}$$ It is $C^1$ everywhere, homogeneous of degree two, and satisfies <a id="eq:transport-reference-gradient"></a>$$\lVert\nabla_WG_0\rVert^2
  =\lVert\nabla_wg_o\rVert^2+\lVert z\rVert^2+\lVert p\rVert^2
  \ge\min(c_o^2,1)\lVert W\rVert^2.
 \label{eq:transport-reference-gradient}$$ Both the value error and its *full* vertical first derivative are uniformly bounded. To verify this explicitly, write <a id="eq:transport-error-decomposition"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 E(X,W)&=\widehat F(X,W)-G_0(X,W)\notag\\
  &=\bigl[F_o(X+\kappa z,w)-F_o(X,w)\bigr]
      +\bigl[F_o(X,w)-g_o(X,w)\bigr]+\sigma(X,p).
 \label{eq:transport-error-decomposition}
\end{aligned}$$ The first bracket and every term in its vertical derivative can be nonzero only with $w,z$ in fixed bounded sets, where the required derivatives of $F_o$ are uniformly bounded. The second bracket and its vertical derivative are bounded because it vanishes for $\lVert w\rVert\ge R_o$. The final term has compact support. The expression $-z\cdot p$, although unbounded, has cancelled exactly. Thus for some finite $M_0,M_1$, <a id="eq:transport-bounded-error"></a>$$\lvert E(X,W)\rvert\le M_0,
 \qquad \lVert\nabla_WE(X,W)\rVert\le M_1
 \quad(X\in\overline U, W\in\mathbb R^{r+2d}).
 \label{eq:transport-bounded-error}$$ No compact-support assertion for $E$ in all its variables is needed.

Apply Lemma <a href="#lem:stabilization-smoothing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:stabilization-smoothing">7</a> with $q'(z,p)=-z\cdot p$, uniformly over $\overline U$. Its homogeneous smoothing is <a id="eq:transport-angular-smoothing"></a>$$G_\eta(X,W)=
   \rho_\eta(\lVert w\rVert/\lVert W\rVert)g_o(X,w)-z\cdot p
 \quad(W\ne0),
 \label{eq:transport-angular-smoothing}$$ where $\rho_\eta$ vanishes for arguments at most $\eta$ and is $1$ for arguments at least $2\eta$. Choose $\eta$ small and then $A$ sufficiently large as in the lemma, with $A$ beyond the uniform critical-locus bound. Let $\tau_A$ be the radial cutoff from that construction and set <a id="eq:transport-final-step-family"></a>$$F_n=(1-\tau_A)\widehat F+\tau_A G_\eta.
 \label{eq:transport-final-step-family}$$ The lemma shows that this function is smooth, equals $G_\eta$ for $\lVert W\rVert\ge2A$, and has no new critical points. It leaves a neighborhood of the entire critical locus unchanged, so $F_n$ has the same regular critical locus and generated parametrization as $\widehat F$. It also supplies a straight homotopy from $G_0$ to $G_\eta$ through regular $C^1$ homogeneous functions.

The new homogeneous function $G_\eta$ is smooth off zero, smoothly dependent on $X$ up to $\overline U$, and has a uniform positive gradient margin on the unit sphere. Homogeneity and compactness there give the next constants of the form <a href="#eq:transport-old-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-old-bounds">[eq:transport-old-bounds]</a>; the remaining uniform smooth bounds follow directly from the construction. The quadratic form $-z\cdot p$ is nondegenerate with signature $(d,d)$: in fixed linear coordinates $z=a+b$, $p=a-b$ it equals $-\lVert a\rVert^2+\lVert b\rVert^2$. The space of nondegenerate forms of signature $(d,d)$ is connected. Choose a fixed path in this space from $-z\cdot p$ to the standard split form in the chosen added coordinates. Its direct sum with $g_o(X,w)$ has nonzero total gradient off the origin, since both block gradients can vanish only there. This path is independent of $X$, so direct sum defines a homotopy over the whole base. Together with the straight homotopy to $G_\eta$, it preserves the free stable tube class on every fixed compact parameter slice. This proves all the assertions. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-21"></a>
#### The final critical point and the contradiction

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-transport-step-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-transport-step</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.* Take the class $a\ne0$ with $\gamma_*a=0$ supplied by Proposition <a href="#prop:kernel-class" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:kernel-class">3</a>, and the closed exact embedded Lagrangian $L\subset T^*(S^9\times S^{N-1})$ supplied by Proposition <a href="#prop:embedded-realization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:embedded-realization">14</a>. Suppose that a Hamiltonian isotopy as in the theorem sent the zero section to $L$. Lemma <a href="#lem:transport-cylinder-extension" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:transport-cylinder-extension">15</a> supplies a compactly supported Euclidean Hamiltonian whose final cylinder is the zero graph over $U_c$. Start with <a href="#eq:transport-initial-family" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-initial-family">[eq:transport-initial-family]</a> and apply Proposition <a href="#prop:transport-step" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:transport-step">17</a> to each of its $s$ increments. Only finitely many steps are required. The subdivision is fixed by the mixed-coordinate and spatial-buffer conditions, which depend on the Hamiltonian and its support. At each step the new family constants change only the logarithmic cutoff, smoothing parameter and outer radius; they impose no further restriction on the step length. Thus the estimates and cutoff radii may be chosen anew at each step. We obtain a smooth family $$F^{(s)}:U\times\mathbb R^{2p}\longrightarrow\mathbb R,
 \qquad p=m+ds,$$ with exactly homogeneous smooth-off-zero data at infinity, whose regular critical locus maps diffeomorphically onto $\widetilde\varphi_1(\mathcal L_0)$.

<a id="eq:transport-final-projection"></a>
Over $U_c$, the full critical-locus projection $$\operatorname{Crit}(F^{(s)})\big|_{U_c}\longrightarrow U_c
 \label{eq:transport-final-projection}$$ is a diffeomorphism: it is the generating parametrization followed by projection of the zero graph. In particular there is precisely one critical point over every $X\in U_c$, and these points form a smooth section. Their vertical Hessians are invertible. Indeed, if $v\in\mathbb R^{2p}$ were in the kernel of such a Hessian, then $$D(\partial_WF^{(s)})_{(X,W)}(0,v)
   =\partial_W^2F^{(s)}(X,W)v=0.$$ Since the full critical equation is regular, its zero set has this kernel as its tangent space. Thus $(0,v)$ would be tangent to $\operatorname{Crit}(F^{(s)})$ and in the kernel of the differential of <a href="#eq:transport-final-projection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-final-projection">[eq:transport-final-projection]</a>. That projection is a diffeomorphism, so $v=0$; the square Hessian is nonsingular.

Fix $v_0\in S^{N-1}$ and restrict the base to $$b\longmapsto\iota((b,v_0),0),\qquad b\in S^9.$$ The resulting smooth family $f_b:\mathbb R^{2p}\to\mathbb R$ therefore has exactly one nondegenerate fiber critical point, varying smoothly with $b$. Its homogeneous data belong to $T_{p,p}$, since each transport step added signature $(d,d)$. On this fixed slice the initial homogeneous family was $g_b$, and every subsequent step preserved its class after split stabilization. Hence, writing $g_\infty:S^9\to\mathbf T$ for the final stable tube family, $$[g_\infty]_{\mathrm{free}}=[a]_{\mathrm{free}}\ne[*],
 \qquad \gamma\circ g_\infty\simeq *.$$ This formulation does not require the smoothing homotopies to fix a chosen basepoint. Equivalently, the basepoint paths supplied by these homotopies identify the resulting based class with $a$. All hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:one-critical" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:one-critical">5</a> hold, including the uniform homogeneous behavior and the required large indices. That proposition makes the same tube family freely null in the stable tube space, contradicting the non-null class of Proposition <a href="#prop:kernel-class" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:kernel-class">3</a>. The assumed compactly supported Hamiltonian isotopy does not exist. This closed exact embedded $L$ is therefore the required counterexample. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-22"></a>

In (a), the dashed segments are the fixed vertical faces on the added incoming collar $Σ × [−1,0] × J$; both sides beyond that collar are movable outgoing faces. In (b), the dark region is $W_{b} = (Y×[−1,0]) ∪ ℛ_{b}$, and its heavy interface with the light complement is $E_{b} = ∂_{+}W_{b}$. The product identification of the complement is proved over the possibly varying bundle $E → B$. The angular direction is schematic: the upper and lower dashed display edges are not boundary faces of the closed sphere $Y$.       "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=0.72cm,y=0.72cm,font=\\small,\n  every node/.style={inner sep=2pt},line cap=round]\n  \\draw[->,black!60] (0,0.5)--(5.3,0.5) node[right] {$v^\\perp$};\n  \\draw[->,black!60] (0,0.5)--(0,4.4) node[above] {$v$};\n  \\draw[densely dashed,black!50] (3.4,0.5)--(3.4,4.03);\n  \\draw[line width=1pt] (3.4,1.48)--(3.4,3.78);\n  \\fill (3.4,1.48) circle (2pt) node[right] {$w'$};\n  \\fill (3.4,3.78) circle (2pt) node[right] {$w$};\n  \\draw[->] (2.97,1.6)--(2.97,3.66)\n    node[midway,left] {$tv$};\n  \\fill[black!60] (3.4,0.5) circle (1.6pt);\n  \\node[anchor=north,align=center,font=\\footnotesize] at (3.4,0.24)\n    {same projection\\\\onto $v^\\perp$};\n  \\node[anchor=west] at (7,3.85) {Equal sphere momenta force};\n  \\node[anchor=west] at (7,3.22) {$w-w'=tv$.};\n  \\node[anchor=west] at (7,2.35) {For $b\\notin U_0$, base momenta satisfy};\n  \\node[anchor=west] at (7,1.55)\n    {$\\|\\Delta p_B\\|\\geq R(b)|t|(K\\delta_0-C_1/c)$.};\n  \\node[anchor=west] at (7,0.7)\n    {$K\\delta_0>C_1/c,\\quad \\Delta p_B=0\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ t=0$.};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "On the left, a 2D Cartesian coordinate system with a vertical axis labeled $v$ with an upward arrow and a horizontal axis labeled $v^\\perp$ with a rightward arrow. Two points $w$ and $w'$ share the same horizontal coordinate, with vertical dashed lines descending to the $v^\\perp$ axis labeled \"same projection onto $v^\\perp$\". A vertical segment between $w'$ and $w$ is marked with bidirectional arrows and labeled $tv$.\nTo the right of the diagram, the following printed text and formulas appear:\n\"Equal sphere momenta force\n$w - w' = tv$.\nFor $b \\notin U_0$, base momenta satisfy\n$\\|\\Delta p_B\\| \\ge R(b)|t|(K\\delta_0 - C_1/c)$.\n$K\\delta_0 > C_1/c, \\quad \\Delta p_B = 0 \\implies t = 0$.\""
